In the 1900s, fur traders hunted chinchillas with dynamite.
The Mexican raccoon is formally known as the coati.
There are four species of coati
The Mexican raccoon is the species known as the white-nosed coati
Coatimundi, quati, tejon, and hog-nosed coon are other names for coatis.
Tarantulas are a common part of the diet of a coati
Coatis are members of the raccoon, or procyonid, family.
The cougar, mountain lion, and puma are all the same animal
Cougars have a lifespan of about 12 to 18 years.
Male cougars are much larger than females
Male cougars weigh 130 to 260 pounds.
Female cougars weigh 80 to 160 pounds.
The average male cougar is larger than the average African leopard
Cougars are not considered "big cats" because they do not roar
There are 19 coyote subspecies across the entire U.S. (including Alaska), Central America, and most of Canada.
Degus are related to chinchillas
Degus are not nocturnal; they are awake during the day.
Degus live in huge colonies of as many as 100
The degu is not tolerant of sugars, and is used in diabetes research
Degus are usually brown, but some pets come in new colors
Degus are very vocal, making chattering, squeaking and chuckling noises
Dingoes are neither dogs nor wolves; they are their own distinct species.
The dingo is also called the warrigal
Dingoes are usually red, but can be black, cream and even white
The dingo is the largest carnivore in Australia
Packs of dingoes can take down a full-grown kangaroo.
Kangaroos have been known to drown dingoes
Dingoes can be found in cave paintings.
The world's longest fence was built to keep dingoes out of southeastern Australia.
Dogs have been domesticated longer than any other animal
There are over 300 breeds of dogs worldwide
Some dogs can recognize over 1000 words
There are approximately 600 million dogs in the world
Dogs range in size from 2 to over 200 pounds
A dog's sense of smell is 10,000 times more powerful than ours.
Bloodhounds don't smell blood; the “blood” in their name refers to bluebloods, because only royalty could once own them.
Yorkshire terriers were bred small to hunt rats and mice in factories, where they could fit under machinery.
The Russian wolfhound, or Borzoi, really did hunt wolves, but Borzois hunted in groups of four or five.
Although the bottlenose is most familiar, there are 41 different species of dolphin!
The killer whale is actually a species of dolphin.
The bottlenose dolphin's top speed is almost 30 mph.
Dolphins sleep with 1/2 of their brain at a time!
Bottlenose dolphins each have their own "whistle" that they use to identify themselves - exactly like a human name
The dolphin is one of the few animals to be born tail first - Dolphin Facts
Hidden under the spines, echidnas have a teeny, tiny tail
A baby echidna is called a "puggle"
The echidna can use its beak like a crowbar to flip stones.
There are 4 species of echidna
The echidna has no external ears, but excellent hearing
Echidnas have electrosensors in their noses
Echidnas blow snot bubbles with their nostrils to keep dust out
Echidnas have no teeth
The Asian elephant is commonly referred to as the Indian elephant.
It is becoming more common for wild African and Asian elephants to reach adulthood without ever growing tusks.
The Asian elephant has much smaller ears than the African elephant and has two distinctive bumps on the top of the head.
Male elk lose their antlers in late winter and grow a new pair in the spring.
Elk have teeth called “ivories” that were once 8-inch-long tusks, like a walrus’s.
Elk antlers may weigh 40 pounds
Elk are ruminants and "chew their cud" just like a cow
A well-organized elk herd can deter a wolf pack attack
Elk are also known by the Shawnee Indian name "wapiti"
Elk ivories are used in jewelry
The elk antler has properties that can fight cancer and other diseases
Elk are raised on farms where their antlers can be safely harvested without harming the elk
The fossa is related to the mongoose
The fossa's favorite prey are lemurs
Fossas are about 20 to 30 inches long without the tail
The tail is usually as long as the body
Fossas weigh 15 to 30 pounds, with males larger.
Fossas are a solid bronze/brown color with brown eyes
The red fox is not always red but ranges in shades from cream to almost black.
A fox's bones are about 30% lighter than the bones of a domestic dog of the same size.
The fox can hear rodents underground.
The grey fox has partially retractable claws - almost like a cat.
The fox is taxonomically a carnivore, but will eat almost anything, including fruit
The "fox hunt" is one of the most common themes in oil paintings, but rarely depicts the fox.
The giraffe is the largest ruminant and the world's tallest animal.
Male giraffes can be 18 feet tall at the top of the horns
Giraffes have 2 to 5 horns called "occicones"
The giraffe's neck is 6 to 8 feet long and can weigh over 600 pounds.
Giraffes "chew their cud" just like cows
Giraffes defend themselves by kicking
A giraffe's kick can kill a lion!
The giraffe's tongue is about 18 inches long.
A group of gorillas is called a "troop"
The gorilla is the largest of the great apes
Adult male gorillas are called "silverbacks" for the silver hair that grows on their backs
Western silverbacks have silver hair down their sides and thighs; Eastern silverbacks have a defined patch of silver between the shoulders.
Adult male gorillas are usually two times the size of adult females.
All gorilla species are critically endangered.
The mountain gorilla was not known to exist until 1902.
Gorillas are almost entirely vegetarian, eating small insects only occasionally.
The grizzly bear can eat about 100 pounds of food a day.
Grizzly bears do not hibernate
Grizzly bears and polar bears occasionally inter-breed in the wild. Some think their reddish-colored offspring are mistaken for Bigfoot!
The grizzly bear's sense of smell is seven times greater than that of a bloodhound.
Grizzly bears stay with their mothers for as long as five years
A new-born baby grizzly bear is smaller than a guinea pig.
The groundhog is also known as the woodchuck
When groundhogs hibernate, their hearts may beat only 4 times a minute.
Other animals, such as skunks, use groundhog burrows as their homes.
Groundhogs use a high-pitched whistle as a warning.
Groundhogs lose over 30% of their body weight during hibernation.
The groundhog is the only rodent with its own day.
Syrian hamsters are also called golden hamsters
Teddy bear hamsters are Syrian hamsters with long hair
Hamsters sleep during the day and are awake at night.
A hamster's teeth never stop growing.
Hamsters must gnaw and chew on hard things to wear down their teeth.
Hamsters can learn their names and be taught very simple tricks
Hedgehogs are not rodents
African pygmy hedgehogs are actually hybrids of two different species
A hedgehog's spikes are called spines or quills.
The quills are made of keratin and are modified hairs.
Hedgehog quills are different from porcupine quills
Hedgehog quills are not barbed at the end, and they don't detach easily.
A group of hippos is called a raft, pod, herd, dale or bloat!
Hippos regularly hold their breath for 5 minutes or more
Baby hippos are usually born underwater and must swim to the surface to take their first breath!
The name "hippopotamus" means "water-horse"
The hippos' tusks never stop growing.
Hippos generally do not eat in the water
Hippos leave the water at night to eat grass
The whale is the closest relative of the hippo
The honey badger has such tough skin that most stingers and many fangs can't penetrate it.
The honey badger is widely recognized as the world's most fearless and fiercest animal.
The honey badger can survive a bite from a king cobra and then eat the snake.
Honey badgers are active day and night and are always hunting
The horse family includes zebras and donkeys
In 1900, approximately 130,000 horses were working in Manhattan.
During the war in Afghanistan, U.S. special forces rode horses to get through the difficult terrain.
Horses have a group of special ligaments called the "stay apparatus" that allow them to sleep standing up
Horses were domesticated about 6,000 years ago
The hyena's heart is massive, allowing it to run at high speeds for miles at a time.
Male hyenas babysit.
The jaguar is the only big cat native to the New World.
The jaguar is the third largest cat species in the world behind only the tiger and the lion.
Presumably the last wild jaguar in the United States was euthanized in 2009
Jaguars are the least likely of all the big cats to attack a human being
Black jaguars are called "black panthers"
Black panthers occur about 6% of the time and can be born in the same litter as normally colored jaguars.
Black panthers have faintly visible spots.
The kangaroo is the largest marsupial
Kangaroos are only about the size of a jellybean at birth
The kangaroo is the only large mammal that moves by hopping
Kangaroos can hop up to 30 feet in one jump
Kangaroos can jump up to 10 feet high
Kangaroos' hind legs always move together, except when they are swimming.
A mother's pouch is called a "marsupium," and is where the word "marsupial" comes from.
The killer whale is actually the largest species of dolphin.
Killer whales stick their heads straight out of the water and inspect the world above the sea in an activity called "spyhopping"
Killer whales are also known as "orcas" and by Inuit communities as "blackfish"
Killer whales' top speed is almost 35 mph.
Killer whales sleep with 1/2 their brain at a time!
The whale has a structure in its brain to process emotions that no other animal has - including human beings
Killer whales may actually be several different species
The killer whale is one of the few animals to be born tail first.
The koala is not a bear and is not related to any bear species
Koalas sleep between 18 and 20 hours a day
Koalas smell like eucalyptus cough drops
Male koalas have a scent gland on their chest that they rub on their home trees to mark them
Male koalas are called bucks, females are does, and babies are joeys.
Lemurs are only found on the island of Madagascar and tiny neighboring Comoro islands
Lemurs are primates, but they are not monkeys
Lemurs have lower front teeth that are fashioned into a comb for grooming
Ring-tailed lemurs spend the most time on the ground
The aye-aye has a special, long, bony finger for hunting insects.
Natives believed that whoever the aye-aye pointed its finger at was doomed.
The lemur has a wet nose, like a dog, and a great sense of smell
Scientists think lemur ancestors drifted to the island of Madagascar on clumps of floating vegetation
The leopard is the best climber of the big cats.
Leopards can drag prey twice their size high up into trees to keep them from scavengers
There are more leopards in the wild than lions, tigers and cheetahs combined.
A leopard's spots are circles called “rosettes,” which may be round or square depending on the region.
Leopards hang their kill in trees.
Although leopards are rarely in groups, a group of them is called a “leap” or a “prowl.”
Leopards can be found in both rainforests and the snow-covered wilderness of Siberia.
Leopards are also known as panthers.
A black panther is a leopard with melanistic coat coloration.
Lions can consume up to 40 pounds of meat in a single meal.
African lions sleep—or doze, rather—about 20 hours a day.
The “big cats” are the tiger, lion, leopard, and jaguar—the only four members of the genus Panthera.
The "big cats" are the only cats who can truly roar.
Big cats roar thanks to a specially designed larynx (a part of the throat).
The African lion has the longest larynx—and the loudest roar.
Llamas have been domesticated for about 5,000 years
The llama has specially shaped blood cells to store oxygen at high altitudes.
Male llamas grow sharp “fighting teeth” and may damage each other if they aren’t removed.
Llamas were used to carry building materials during the construction of Machu Picchu.
Llama herds hum in unison
A baby llama is called a cria
Alpacas and llamas often interbreed, and the result is called a huarizo
Some alpacas are worth hundreds of thousands of dollars due to their exceptional wool, called “fiber.”
Alpaca fiber is softer than cashmere
Christopher Columbus mistook a manatee for a mermaid
Manatees may have the smallest brain-to-body ratio of any mammal
When sleeping, manatees only need to take 3 breaths an hour
Algae often grow on the backs of manatees and act as a sunscreen.
Manatees have thousands of whisker-like hairs all over their bodies
The elephant is the closest relative of the manatee
Manatees use their flippers to walk along the river bottom
Manatees replace their molars as they wear down.
Despite appearances, manatees do not have a layer of blubber and cannot tolerate cold.
The meerkat is also called the suricate
The meerkat is not a weasel; it is a species of mongoose.
Like all mongooses, meerkats can kill cobras
Meerkats have about 20 specific warning calls
A group of meerkats is called a "mob", a "gang", a "pack", or a "troop".
Meerkats have bare patches on their bellies that they expose to the sun when they need to warm up
Scorpions are the meerkat's favorite food.
A group of mongooses is called a "mob," a "pack," a "gang," or a "troop."
The mongoose is not a weasel
The meerkat is a species of mongoose
Some mongoose species live in abandoned termite mounds
The mongoose has horizontal pupils.
Most mongoose species have a very high tolerance for scorpion and snake venom.
A group of monkeys is called a "troop"
Some Old World monkeys have cheek pouches that allow them to save food and chew it later.
Many monkey species, like capuchins, use tools regularly.
Only one monkey species exists in Europe, the Barbary macaque of Gibraltar
Howler monkeys can be heard for 5 miles
Some New World monkeys may live their entire lives in rainforest treetops and never touch the ground.
The moose is the largest deer in the world
Moose antlers fall off every winter and grow back every spring.
Moose mothers usually have twins
A full-grown male moose can be 7 feet tall at the shoulder.
A moose's large nose helps it eat with its head underwater.
The moose has a fleshy, hanging dewlap called a "bell"
Moose antlers can be over 6 feet wide
Moose may perish stuck together if their antlers lock during a fight.
Mustangs average about 56 inches at the shoulder
There are approximately 50,000 wild mustangs
There is controversy over whether mustangs are feral or truly wild.
Mustangs can run up to 35 miles an hour
Mustangs have one of the longest life spans of horse breeds, sometimes reaching 40 years!
The okapi is the closest relative of the giraffe
An okapi's tongue is so long that it can lick its ears.
Okapis have 2 skin-covered horns called "occicones".
The okapi has scent glands in its feet that leave a stinky residue.
Okapis "chew their cud" just like cows
The okapi can communicate using sounds that humans can't hear.
Opossum rabies is actually rarer than rabies in skunks, raccoons, or foxes.
The opossum is often called a possum, with the "o" omitted.
Old World "possums" and New World "opossums" are not closely related
Opossums have prehensile tails and opposable big toes
The opossum is not a rodent; it is a marsupial like a kangaroo.
Opossums raise their young in a pouch called a "marsupium"
The common opossum is the only marsupial in North America
Opossums "play opossum" or "play dead" to confuse predators
A group of orangutans is called a "congress"
The orangutan is the only great ape native to Asia
Orangutans are the largest tree-dwelling animals in the world
An orangutan has incredibly strong arms that are twice as long as its legs
Adult male orangutans develop large "cheek flanges" that are thick pads of fat that frame their faces
The orangutan is the only great ape that does not live in large groups
Orangutans share about 96.4% of our DNA
Orangutans love a fruit called durian, which smells like strong garlic.
Giant otters eat piranhas
River otters love to body surf in the snow
Mother sea otters wrap their babies in kelp or seaweed while they dive
Otter poop has its own special name: “spraint.”
The sea otter has the densest coat of any mammal
Sea otter fur has one million individual hairs per square inch
Sea otters use rocks as tools to break open clams and crab shells
The sea otter is the smallest marine mammal
Fishermen in Bangladesh used trained otters to help them net the catch of the day
The giant panda's diet is over 95% bamboo
A newborn panda weighs only 3 to 5 ounces and is 1/1000th the size of its mother!
The red panda or "firefox" is more closely related to a ferret than to a bear.
There are over one billion pigs on Earth
Pigs can squeal almost as loud as a jet engine
Pigs can smell things 7 miles away and 20 feet underground
Pigs do have a few sweat glands, mostly on their noses
Pigs use their snouts for digging
A pig's hooves are called trotters
Pigs will eat almost anything, including feces
Ancient Europeans once used pigs to get rid of sewage
The polar bear is the largest land carnivore
Polar bears and brown bears occasionally inter-breed in the wild. Some think their reddish-colored offspring are mistaken for Bigfoot!
A polar bear's fur is not white; the hairs are actually translucent.
Polar bears can smell a seal coming up for air over 1/2 a mile away.
Porcupines can't throw their quills
Porcupines have a strong, musky "wet dog" smell
Though they are a little clumsy, porcupines are built for climbing
African Cape porcupines have 20-inch quills.
Porcupine quills are hollow
Porcupine guard hairs are used to make a Native American headdress called a "roach".
The quills have barbs on the tips that cause them to burrow deeper into the skin and make them hard to remove.
The fisher, a very tough weasel species, regularly eats porcupines
Some porcupines have prehensile tails like New World monkeys
Puppies are born blind and hairless
Their eyes will open at about 8 to 14 days
Puppies can stand up at about 15 days old
They are usually able to walk about by 21 days old
Puppies are born with 28 "puppy teeth"
A group of rabbits is called a "herd"
Rabbits live in underground burrows called "warrens" that can include several rooms and several hundred feet of tunnels
A male rabbit is a buck, a female is a doe, and a baby is a kit, kitten, or bunny.
Rabbits are lagomorphs, not rodents.
Domestic rabbits have changed in captivity so substantially that they can no longer successfully breed with wild rabbits
Rabbits can see completely behind them
A rabbit's teeth continue to grow throughout its entire life.
Rabbit meat is leaner than beef, chicken, or pork, and is all white meat.
A group of raccoons is called a "nursery" or a "gaze" of raccoons
The word raccoon comes from a Powhatan Native American word meaning "scrubs with hands"
Raccoons were once thought to be tiny bears.
Raccoons have whiskers on their front toes!
Raccoons do not have opposable thumbs, but their "pinkie" finger and thumb can meet across the palm.
Raccoons are born with very fine fuzz and faint little masks around their eyes.
The "raccoon dog" looks remarkably like a raccoon but is not related to the raccoon.
The reindeer of North America are called "caribou"
The Eurasian reindeer is the only domestic species of deer
Reindeer are the only species of deer in which the females usually have antlers
The reindeer has the largest antlers relative to body mass of all deer, including moose.
A mature male forest reindeer can have an antler spread of over 5 feet
Some caribou herds have the longest migration of any land animal - over 3000 miles
White rhinos and black rhinos are both actually grey in color.
The white rhinos are probably called "white" due to a misinterpretation of the word for "wide," referring to their wide, straight lips.
The black rhino may be called black because it often covers itself with very dark mud.
The largest rhino horns can be over 4 feet in length!
Rhinoceros skin is over an inch thick in some parts of the body
Rhinos are not nearly as dangerous as they look, and only about 3 attacks a year are reported.
Seals are known as the true seals, the earless seals or the crawling seals
A male seal is called a bull, a female is called a cow, and a baby is called a pup
A group of seals is called a colony on land, and a raft when in the water.
Some seals can stay underwater for up to an hour without taking a breath
Seals replace their skin and fur every year in a single 30-day molt.
Sea lions are known as eared or walking seals
A male sea lion is called a bull, a female is called a cow, and a baby is called a pup
A group of sea lions is called a colony on land, and a raft when in the water.
Some sea lions can stay underwater for over half an hour without taking a breath
Male sea lions may fast for up to 40 days during mating season.
Female sea lions may fast for up to 16 days when their pup is first born
The trained seals seen in water parks and circuses are usually California sea lions.
Servals are bred with domestic housecats to produce Savannah cats.
The serval eats rodents and small birds whole
Serval cats have the biggest ears of any cat species
A serval's tail is about half the length of a domestic cat's tail.
Servals are the most efficient hunters of all cat species
Servals are successful in 50 percent of their hunts
Lions are only successful about 30 percent of the time
Skunks can accurately spray up to 15 feet
Skunk spray is not urine; it consists of secretions from the anal glands.
A skunk's bold coloring is a warning to stay away.
Skunks almost never spray each other, except during mating season.
The only thing that eats skunks regularly is the Great horned owl
Great horned owls have a very poor sense of smell!
Skunks are immune to rattlesnake venom, bee stings and scorpions, and will eat all of them
Some people keep skunks as pets
The sloth has the slowest metabolism of any mammal on Earth.
Sloths take about 25 days to digest one leaf.
The ancestors of today's sloths were as big as African elephants.
Both two-toed and three-toed sloths have three toes on their hind limbs.
The sloth takes a potty break only once a week
Sloths sleep hanging completely upside-down
A sloth's fur is home to algae, lichen, and even moths.
The sloth turns green during the rainy season because algae grow on its fur.
The snow leopard has been considered a "big cat" since 2008
Unlike other big cats, snow leopards cannot roar.
Snow leopards are 22 to 24 inches tall at the shoulder
Snow leopards weigh between 60 and 100 pounds
Snow leopards' large feet spread out and act like snowshoes.
Snow leopards are nocturnal, and hunt at night
Snow leopards can jump a distance of over 30 feet.
Squirrels can remember the location of hundreds of buried nuts
The squirrel is a rodent, related to marmots and prairie dogs
Squirrels have double-jointed ankles so they can come down a tree head first
A squirrel's ankles rotate so its toe claws can hook onto tree bark.
There are dozens of species of squirrels found all over the world
Squirrels make a nest out of twigs called a drey
Tarsiers can move their heads 180 degrees in either direction
The ankle bones, or tarsals, of the tarsier are incredibly long
The tarsier is named for its unique tarsal bones
A tarsier's hind legs are longer, relative to its body, than those of any other mammal.
Tarsiers can jump about 30 times their body length
U.S. Navy Squadron VF 33, from NAS Oceana, Va., has the tarsier as its squadron mascot and insignia.
Tarsier fossils are 50 million years old
The tarsier makes noises so high in frequency that we can't hear them
A Tasmanian devil's jaws open a full 80 degrees, and it has a bite strength of 1200 PSI.
Tasmanian devils spend more time biting each other than any other species
The huge head, massive neck, and powerful jaws allow Tasmanian devils to eat parts of carcasses that others might leave behind.
The Siberian tiger is also known as the Amur tiger.
The tiger is the largest of the four "Big Cats"
Tigers love playing, swimming, and snoozing in water.
There are 3 times as many tigers kept as personal pets in the U.S. as there are in the wild.
Tigers can eat 60 pounds of meat at one time.
A group of tigers is called a "streak"
Tigers can be found on tropical islands and in the snow-covered wilderness of Siberia.
The walrus can dive to depths of over 300 feet
Walruses use their tusks to pull themselves up onto icebergs
The tusks of a male walrus can grow up to 40 inches
Walruses have 1-inch-thick skin.
Walruses have 6-inch-thick blubber.
The walrus's Latin name means “tooth-walker.”
Walruses live in the oceans around the North Pole
The walrus eats hundreds of clams a day
Wildebeest are energetic, active and playful
A wildebeest can run at 50 miles per hour
There are 2 species of wildebeest: blue wildebeest and black wildebeest.
Wildebeest have a lifespan of up to 20 years
Both male and female wildebeests have horns
Some 2 million wildebeest take part in the great migration each year
Not all populations of wildebeests migrate
Wolves are born with blue eyes
When packs howl, they harmonize
The wolf is the largest species of canine
Wolves can be solid black and solid white as well as all shades of grey and brown
It is a myth that wolf howls don't echo
A wolf's sense of smell is 100 times more powerful than ours
A wolf pack's size seems to be determined by the size of game available.
Wolves regularly hunt animals like moose that may be 10 times their size
Wolves lift their legs to urinate on trees just like dogs
Wolverines have poor eyesight but a great sense of smell
They can smell a dead animal buried 10 or even 20 feet below the snow
A 30-pound wolverine can take down a 150-pound deer.
Wolverines have special teeth for slicing through frozen flesh
Wolverine fur does not collect frost or ice and is used to line parkas and hoods
The wolverine has 4-inch-long claws.
The average lifespan of a wolverine is about 10 years.
When the busy day catches up with them, wombats will nap lying flat on their backs.
Wombats are most closely related to koalas but have much larger brains.
Wombats are quite accurately nicknamed “the bulldozers of the bush.”
A newborn wombat is the size of a jellybean!
A mother wombat’s pouch is called a “marsupium,” and the word “marsupial” comes from it.
Wombats are at home on the beach
A wombat’s teeth never stop growing.
A wombat’s pouch faces backward, not forward like a kangaroo’s.
A wombat’s den can be 150 feet long and have 5 different exits and entrances.
Male wombats are Jacks, female wombats are Jills, and baby wombats are Joeys.
A wombat’s droppings are cube-shaped.
Wombats do snore.
Contrary to some claims, zebras, like all other equines, identify each other by smell and vocalization, not by their stripes.
Zebras' stripes make it more difficult for predators to target one individual in the herd.
Aardvarks are well adapted for heat and have spare body hair, with each follicle grouped into clusters of three or four.
Aardvark babies resemble Dobby from Harry Potter, with droopy ears and hairless, wrinkled skin.
Aardwolves are prolific scent-markers, leaving scent trails throughout their territory.
Aardwolves are shy, secretive, and do not do well in captivity.
Aardwolves can have up to 10 burrows in a single territory.
Although they live in some of the drier savannah areas, aardwolves rarely drink water.
The sex of juvenile alligators depends on the temperature of the nest where their eggs are laid.
Their teeth continuously fall out and regrow. Over an alligator’s lifetime, it can go through 2,000 to 3,000 teeth.
Alligators can perform a ‘death roll’ by gripping prey in their jaws and spinning until bite-sized chunks are torn off.
The largest ‘gator ever recorded was 14ft 9, around 4.5 m. The male was killed in Alabama in 2014.
Alpine Ibex have backward-curving horns with ridges that can grow up to 55 inches (140 cm).
Ibex can jump more than 2 metres (6 feet) straight up without running.
Their names reflect their shells. In Spanish, the armadillo’s name translates to “little armored one”.
During the day, armadillos shelter in underground burrows that can be 20 feet long.
Armadillos often sleep 16 hours a day and are solitary animals.
Asian honeybees have developed a defensive ‘bee ball’ mechanism against giant hornets.
Western honeybees have no defence. Unfortunately, honeybees in Europe and the US are a different species, Apis melifera.
Atlantic wolffish come in a variety of colours, with skin ranging from blue-grey to green and purple.
They don’t eat. At least, once they emerge as adults, that’s the end of their feeding.
They have a high mortality rate. Moths, like most (if not all) insects, reproduce using what’s known as R-strategy.
The larvae have butt cannons that provide a very irritating defense when threatened.
Adult Atlas moths have enormous, dappled wings said to resemble a snake.
The Aye-aye is one of only two animal species that hunts for food using ‘percussive foraging.’
Some species can go days without water. The chacma baboon lives in drier habitats that resemble desert environments.
Baboons love to talk. Using approximately a dozen variations, baboons will use a combination of vocalizations to express themselves.
Humans share about 94 percent of our genes with baboons. In terms of genetic likeness, baboons are closely related to humans.
Bald eagles are notorious thieves. The osprey, a much smaller fish-eating raptor, often shares the same habitat as bald eagles.
Bald eagles can swim! They will sometimes try to catch fish that are too large to pull out of the water.
Bald eagles are an endangered-species success story; in 1963, researchers estimated just 400 pairs remained in the US.
They are visual hunters. Barracudas have excellent eyesight, and are drawn to the silvery flashes and sudden movements made by their prey.
Their range is expanding. Rising ocean temperatures have allowed many tropical species, including barracuda, to expand their natural range.
Their eyes can rotate. Two green glowing orbs can be seen within the transparent head.
Barreleyes are colorblind; their eyes lack cone cells, which are used for color perception.
The barreleye family contains other bizarre-looking species, such as Winteria telescopa, the binocular fish.
They are expert divers. They are able to withstand great pressures and have been found at depths of over 3,000 feet.
Little is known about their reproduction. Despite their size, not much is known regarding the reproductive behaviors of basking sharks.
They’re bone-breakers. Bearded vultures eat mostly bone, but they have no teeth to break them up.
Beavers have iron teeth. You might have seen eye-catching headlines about beavers having metal teeth, and that’s sort of true.
Beavers can chew down large trees. They are capable of gnawing huge tree trunks and felling large trees.
International Beavers Day is celebrated on April 7th each year.
They are ecosystem engineers. Bilbies move a greater volume of soil than any other animal in their habitat, including introduced rabbits.
Bilbies have extremely short gestation times: 12 to 14 days, among the shortest of any mammal.
They smell like buttered popcorn. Binturongs produce secretions from glands at the base of their tails.
Some people keep them as pets. In parts of Malaysia, binturongs are kept as pets.
They’re not actually black. Although the black rhinoceros is referred to as ‘black’, its colours actually vary from brown to grey.
They wait patiently for their prey. Black widow spiders create a specific type of web called “tangle webs”.
Males are sometimes eaten after mating. For male widow spiders, mating can be extremely dangerous.
They are solitary arthropods. Black widow spiders generally only come together to mate.
They are “comb-footed spiders”. These spiders belong to the Theridiidae family, a group known as “comb-footed spiders”.
They are not aggressive. Contrary to popular belief, black widow spiders are not aggressive.
Their eyesight is poor. Despite having eight eyes (arranged in two rows of four), black widow spiders do not have good eyesight.
Their webs have specialized areas. The webs crafted by black widow spiders have various sections that serve different functions.
There are false widow spiders too. They belong to the genus Steatoda and are often mistaken for black widow spiders.
They take part in 3D migrations. During the cold months, blanket octopuses migrate from deeper water into shallows for warmth.
Blue-footed boobies display sexual dimorphism: females are, on average, 20 to 30 percent larger than males.
Their venom is active even after death. Picking up a blue sea dragon can result in a painful sting.
They know to avoid traffic. While in these developed spaces, they’ve been documented avoiding roads.
Following such a slow reproductive cycle, blue whale families are small. Females usually give birth to just one calf.
A blue whale calf grows fast. It will gain roughly 90 kilograms of weight per day in its first year of life.
They’re easy to hunt. Like most large Arctic animals, these ancient behemoths swim slowly and are covered in thick blubber.
They rank third among species causing unprovoked attacks on humans, behind the great white shark and the tiger shark.
They are both bigger and smaller than alligators. Five of the six caiman species are, in general, smaller than alligators.
They are largely nocturnal. Caimans will spend most of the daytime lying in the sun or cooling off in the waters.
They will eat almost anything. As apex predators, caimans will eat almost anything that they can fit into their mouths.
Prey are swallowed whole. Like crocodiles, caiman cannot chew.
They quickly replace old teeth. Over time, caiman teeth can become worn down and old.
Caimans have exceptional hearing. While their ears may be hard to find, they have excellent hearing abilities.
Their humps are not for storing water. Contrary to popular belief, camel humps do not contain water—they contain fat.
Camels can be divided into three species. There are three species of camel.
They walk like horses. Camels engage in a specific type of gait called pacing.
Their milk is very nutritious! In India, camel milk is growing in popularity due to its nutritional properties.
Camels ‘spit’ when bothered. It is true that camels spit when troubled or when they feel threatened.
Camels are very social animals. Like cows and sheep, camels live in herds.
Camels are expert foragers. Camels are diurnal, meaning they are most active during the daytime.
They mate sitting down. Camels mate with both the male and female sitting down, with the male behind.
Fewer than 1,000 wild Bactrian camels remain alive today; the species is critically endangered.
They can run as fast as 35 kph. They are also extremely agile, which helps them escape predators.
Coatis are all-terrain foragers. Equipped with powerful claws, swivelling ankles and a long tail for balance, coatis are adept climbers.
Male coatis are mostly solitary. Once they have reached maturity, male coatis leave their group and adopt a largely solitary lifestyle.
Coatis give birth in trees. Excellent climbers, females build nests there.
Coatis are threatened by hunting and habitat loss. Like many tropical mammal species, hunting of coatis for food is largely unregulated.
Coelacanths can unhinge their jaws to consume large prey.
Coelacanths have a “spine” that modern-day yogis would envy; instead of a backbone, they have a notochord.
Coelacanths taste awful, so don't expect this prehistoric fish to make a tasty meal with fries.
Colossal squid are the heaviest squid; giant squid have been measured longer.
Their eggs are tiny! For such a gargantuan creature, the colossal squid lays tiny eggs.
They’re cannibals. One of the reasons for hatching colossal squid eggs in the shallows may be to avoid predation from other squid.
They have comb-like teeth. Their teeth look like a tiny comb. These are excellent at slicing through leaves for food.
Baby colugos weigh just 35 g when they are born. This is tiny!
Cougars have more names than any other animal, even in English: mountain lion, puma, and cougar.
Cougars are not apex predators; they are not at the top of the food chain.
A crocodile’s jaws can apply 5,000 pounds of pressure per square inch
Parasitic behavior has evolved independently in a remarkable number of bee lineages.
Cuckoo bees don’t collect pollen and show relative irresponsibility when it comes to brood care.
Some of the most strikingly beautiful cuckoo bees belong to the genus Thyreus.
Cuckoo bees are prevalent, and some bumble bees also exhibit cuckoo behavior.
Dolphins communicate with clicks and whistles, helping them navigate, warn of potential predators, and hunt for fish together.
Dolphins are excellent swimmers and can dive up to 1,000 feet.
Dolphins have been observed caring for old, sick, and injured members of their pod.
Depending on the species, gestation can last from nine to 17 months.
The colonies are nomadic, unlike those of most ant species, which are fixed in place.
Dugongs belong to the same order as manatees and live exclusively in marine habitats.
Dugongs appear to have a constant smile, making them great for selfies.
Dugongs are vulnerable to extinction; their populations are estimated to have declined by 90% in the last 30 years.
Dumbo octopuses pounce on tasty prey and swallow it whole.
Octopuses adapted to highly pressurized environments would not survive in manmade tanks.
Earthworms can breathe underwater because they breathe through their skin.
Earthworms don’t have eyes, although some worms do; sight is unnecessary in dark soil.
Earthworms make group decisions and get along despite living at high densities.
Some worms clone themselves, and up to 40% don’t need to mate.
They completely regurgitate the shell after its contents pass through the egg-eating snake's stomach.
They can climb trees, which helps make these snakes successful hunters.
They are completely harmless and pose no threat to humans; all Dasypeltis species are nonvenomous and rarely bite.
Elephants can curl up together to sleep; a drone captured this family sleeping together.
Emperor penguins are expert divers and the deepest-diving birds known to humans.
Emperor penguins live on “fast ice”. Fast ice is ice that is attached to land or ice shelves.
Males are responsible for incubation. They are the only species to breed during the Antarctic winter.
After approximately seven weeks, chicks cluster together in groups called “creches” as they mature.
Their origin story stems from a travelling farmworker: John Tinsley brought four fainting goats to Tennessee in the 1880s.
The Fangtooth's muscles suggest a powerful bite and the ability to move quickly and powerfully.
Fangtooths, like many deep-sea fishes, remain something of a mystery.
Their name means “flame-colored,” referring to their pinkish-red feathers; the term comes from Spanish or Portuguese.
Females lay only one egg at a time and most often lay one egg after mating.
Chicks and parents recognize each other through distinct vocalizations, and chicks mingle in large groups called creches.
The flamingo is the national bird of The Bahamas. The Bahamas has one of the largest populations of flamingos in the world.
Bats engage in belly ‘dipping’ and often try to cool down by fanning themselves while resting.
One flying snake species makes a pretty good pet: the Paradise Tree Snake grows to about 3 feet long.
The smallest flying snake is the rarest: the Twin-Barred Tree Snake.
They can glide in only one direction; flying snakes cannot fly upward.
Fossa use scent as an important communication tool and have two prominent scent glands.
Fossa males and females only come together to mate in September and October, during a short breeding season.
Jellyfish are edible. People living off the coast of China have been eating jellyfish for at least the past 1700 years.
Frilled sharks have over 300 teeth, and their mouths are adapted to catch and hold soft-bodied prey like squid.
Ghost sharks are invertebrate connoisseurs. Chimaeras are equipped with specialised teeth to suit their diet of benthic invertebrates.
Chimaeras have no stomachs; their simplified digestive system lacks a well-defined stomach.
Their evolutionary origins are mysterious. Chimaera fossils are very rare, and this makes puzzling out their phylogeny very difficult.
Trawling is the main threat faced by ghost sharks. Chimaeras are commonly brought up in deep ocean trawl nets as bycatch.
Mites aren’t the only creatures that infest millipedes.
The giant anteater is the largest type of anteater, and its name reflects that.
They can flick their tongues in and out 160 times per minute, nearly three times per second.
Scolopendra have killed people, but only a handful of deaths have been attributed to their venom.
Males are very aggressive. Male giant forest hogs will often fight for dominance in the group.
Like many pigs, giant forest hogs lack sweat glands and cannot sweat.
They huddle together for warmth. When it is cold, especially at night, individuals in the group will often huddle together for warmth.
They have comb-like teeth called pectines. The giant forest scorpion has anywhere from 16 to 20 little comb-like teeth known as pectines.
Panda mothers and cubs stick together
They’re gigantic: a comment on their impressive size and a reference to the biological adaptation known as gigantism.
These worms live in extreme conditions at the bottom of the deep blue sea.
Giraffes are the tallest mammals on the planet.
Giraffes give birth standing up.
Giraffes’ spots are like fingerprints, though they may not have fingerprints like humans, primates, and koalas.
Birds are not its preferred food, despite the name; very few cases of this spider eating birds are confirmed.
Goliath Birdeater spiderlings are nearly 0.75 inches long, unlike most tiny baby spiders.
Pocket gophers are solitary. They live a quiet, lonely life underground.
They can close their mouths behind their teeth, avoiding hazards when using their faces as shovels.
Gophers dig extensively and are considered ecosystem engineers, earning them the nickname “nature’s rototillers.”
Gorillas are poor swimmers and avoid rivers and large bodies of water because they cannot swim naturally.
They infrequently bite boats
Great White sharks can swim up to 56 kph (35 mph), thanks to their streamlined, torpedo-like bodies.
Their meat is toxic. This is one fish that you won’t want to make into sushi.
Greenland sharks are blind. Most of the rare fish in the sea are born blind.
Green anacondas have been known for cannibalism. Females (the larger of the sexes) have been reported to eat smaller male anacondas.
Green anacondas don’t eat people
They have a distinctive hump on their backs, a large muscle that controls their forearms.
Grizzlies’ claws are as long as human fingers, reaching up to 4 inches long.
Mama bears are fierce; mothers with young cubs protect them, even fighting off larger male bears.
Grizzlies and wolves are competitors; where they overlap, such as Yellowstone Park, they often compete fiercely for food.
All 18 species of hedgehog can roll into a ball for protection.
Baby hedgehogs are called ‘hoglets’. After birth, their quills will start to grow and harden.
As an added layer of protection, hedgehogs “anoint” themselves. Hedgehogs are immune to a range of poisonous plants.
Helmeted hornbills have bare throat pouches; males have bright red, and females have light turquoise-colored throat pouches, or gular sacks.
A hippo can live to become a senior citizen; its average lifespan is between 40 and 50 years.
Hippos engage in “muck-spreading,” marking territory with their own faeces.
The IUCN lists hippos as vulnerable. Their population is thought to be stable, estimated at between 115,000 and 130,000 animals.
This pygmy bat’s wingspan is no more than 4 inches (10 cms); it really is tiny.
Its species name “alba” comes from Latin “albus,” meaning “white,” and refers to its distinctive bright white fur.
Honey badgers have incredibly tough skin. When attacking snakes or bees, it helps to have very thick skin.
Their tough, loose skin helps them in combat and hangs loosely on their bodies.
Honey badgers never give up; they are very determined and tenacious animals.
Their name comes from the howl-like sound they make. This sound is low, guttural and quite scary when heard in the rainforest.
Only male howler monkeys “howl”. Females never make these loud calls.
Their tails have tactile pads that let them feel, much like they do with their hands.
Mayan culture viewed them as gods. The Mayans often depicted howler monkeys as gods in sculptures and they featured in various myths.
They are capable of “swing-hovering” or “side-slipping”. This is the ability to move rapidly from side-to-side while hovering.
Their wings beat at 70-80 times a second. They even emit an audible humming noise as they hover over plants.
Their entire genome is known, and genetics has intrigued the scientific community since their discovery.
Black eagles attack hyraxes from above, and hyraxes make up 98% of black eagles’ diet.
They have sweaty feet. Rock hyraxes have rubbery feet, laden with sweat glands.
Hyraxes have a special grooming claw. Their toes have rounded, hoof-like nails, except for the inner rear toe.
There are only 25 scorpions that have potentially fatal venom. This is out of almost 2,000 species of scorpion.
They are tiny: just 5–9 cm long, making them hard to spot.
They are fluorescent under black light. Their exoskeletons contain chemicals that glow under black light.
They give birth to live young, called “scorplings”. These tiny scorpions are white and cannot sting.
They are kept as pets. Despite their deadly nature, they are commonly kept as pets.
They lay 12–24 eggs in abandoned burrows, where the eggs take 2 months to hatch.
Irrawaddy dolphins are known for their short noses. Part of their scientific name, brevirostris, means “short-beaked.”
They can fire stingers. Irukandji jellyfish have the ability to fire stingers from the tips of their tentacles and inject venom.
A jellyfish’s diet changes as it matures; in the polyp stage, it filter-feeds on zooplankton.
Irukandji have many eyes; the Irukandi jellyfish has up to 24 eyes grouped to perform different functions.
Jaribu means “swollen neck” in the South American Tupi–Guaraní language, referring to its distinctive bulging neck.
When food is scarce, jabirus sometimes steal prey from other birds, including other jabirus.
Jaguars are obligate carnivores and are known to hunt over 85 different prey species.
Jaguars naturally avoid humans and are statistically the least likely big cats to kill and eat them.
They resemble weasels and otters, with elongated bodies, short legs, long tails, and small, squished faces.
They are usually solitary; pairs are sometimes found and are likely mothers with older offspring or mating pairs.
Their closest relative is the cougar, which is much bigger than the medium-size jaguarundi.
They range from Argentina to Mexico.
These cats often inhabit rainforests and can swim across medium-sized rivers.
They can leap as high as 2m into the air when hunting birds.
They are vocal, with 13 recorded calls, including screaming, purring, whistling, and yapping.
They often eat domestic chickens, leading to conflict with farmers.
They have litters of up to 4 kittens, which live in dens in dense undergrowth.
More dominant Japanese macaques get better sleeping spots, usually in trees with good leaf cover.
Japanese macaques teach each other skills; ‘Imo’ was observed washing dirt from potatoes in rivers.
Recent studies suggest they develop different regional “accents,” similar to human culture.
Males are typically bigger and have larger claws, called chelipeds, to fend off predators and opposing crabs.
Japanese spider crabs pass through a larval stage after hatching and before adulthood.
Like other crustaceans, Japanese spider crabs molt as they grow, shedding their exoskeletons.
Females have “aprons”. Female Japanese crab abdomens have also been termed “aprons”.
They are gentle giants. Despite their intimidating and spiky appearance, these crabs are quite calm and slow in nature.
They can leap 3 m to help them avoid predators.
Jerboas can hop as fast as 24 kph, helping them avoid nocturnal predators like owls, foxes, and snakes.
They have big ears and excellent hearing, which helps them avoid predators.
Jerboas don’t drink water; like many desert animals, they get all the water they need from what they eat.
They are some of the fastest-swimming marine mammals and can swim as fast as 56 km/h, around 35 mph.
They go through menopause; only 3 species do: humans, short-finned pilot whales, and Orcas.
King cobras can flatten their bodies to swim, though they aren’t considered aquatic snakes and are often found in water.
King cobras climb faster than humans; the ground isn’t the only place to watch for them.
Experts have named the king cobra the smartest snake in existence.
Kinkajous have very sensitive hearing and can detect snakes slithering toward them.
Koalas aren’t bears. One of the most common names for a koala that you may have heard is the “koala bear”.
They can also swim. Not only do they move fast on land, but they are excellent swimmers.
They’re jawless. Lampreys are members of the taxonomic group called Agnathans, which literally translates to jawless.
They don’t have scales or gill covers. These are technically fish, but they don’t have scales.
They have no paired appendages; these fish don’t really have limbs.
They have no bones. It’s beginning to look like these animals are better described by what they aren’t than what they are.
They technically can’t swim. Despite living in water, their propulsion methods aren’t traditional.
They can occupy saltwater and freshwater. Lampreys inhabit cool waters both in the sea and in freshwater rivers and lakes.
Lampreys are regicidal. In the 12th century, they were popular as a food source.
They do not hibernate. Unlike other rodents, lemmings do not hibernate in the winter and remain active all year round.
Lemmings can swim. When populations grow too large, lemmings may try to swim to new locations in search of new homes.
They like to be alone. Lemmings are not social creatures and prefer to keep to themselves.
Leopards are skilled at going airborne. Leopards may not have wings, but that doesn’t mean they’re stuck on the ground!
The English name ‘leopard’ means ‘spotted lion’ in Latin. It was derived from Old French and Latin.
They are the only seal that hunts warm-blooded prey; other seals hunt only cold-blooded prey, such as fish, shrimp, and krill.
Lesula is a local name. The common name for these monkeys comes from the name the locals give them.
They’re probably polygynous. Most recorded groups comprised primarily females, while many lone males were also spotted.
Lesula exhibit ‘terrestrial quadrupedalism.’ This means they walk on the ground on all fours.
Lions and tigers don’t overlap in the wild today, except in one location.
Lions have a relatively short gestation period: about four months before a young lion is born.
Their scales have a unique shape. Lionfish have cycloid scales that are thin and oval-shaped with smooth edges.
They are ravenous carnivores. Lionfish will eat pretty much anything they can get their mouths on.
They usually hunt at night. Lionfish are believed to be nocturnal creatures, preferring to hunt at night.
Their expansion is truly unprecedented. To many marine biologists, the rate at which lionfish numbers have grown is astounding.
Lungfish need to breathe air to survive. They have gills and lungs, but must breathe air.
They have exceptional ears. Lynx are all equipped with little tufts on the tips of their ears.
They’re related to elephants; the elephant is the other animal in this clade.
Manatees can talk through squeaks and squeals, and sometimes chirps and grunts.
Florida manatees are classified as vulnerable and have low genetic diversity, putting them at risk of collapse.
Manatees often ingest plastic and have died from it on numerous occasions.
‘Mandrill’ derives from ‘Man’ and ‘Drill,’ a West African word meaning ‘baboon’ or ‘ape.’
One study found 1,350 mandrills crossing a road together; some mandrill hordes have over 1,000 members.
Mandrills bond by grooming one another, often to remove fleas.
Mandrills spend less time in trees than other monkeys; their forearms and hindlimbs are almost equal in length.
Like vultures, these storks have long necks and bald heads.
Marabou stork legs are often covered in their white faeces.
Marabou storks may appear to spend much of their time standing around, but they are not lazy.
Margays can run down trees headfirst, thanks to their strong paws and slender bodies.
Margays are largely solitary, coming together only to mate.
Despite ‘kat’ in their name, meerkats are not related to cats.
Meerkats, members of the mongoose family, are immune to certain types of venom.
A meerkat’s vision is its most developed sense.
Meerkats have no body fat, which explains why they are so small.
Meerkats have specialized thermoregulation that helps them survive their harsh, dry desert habitat.
Meerkats have been widely portrayed in film and television.
Its body is weak because it lacks a strong muscle structure.
It likes variety in its diet. Studies show that it feeds on krill, copepods, and gelatinous zooplankton.
It is still a little-known species. Studies have examined its systematics, feeding ecology, and swimming behaviour.
The plural of “mongoose” is “mongooses,” not “mongeese,” though “mongoose” is also acceptable.
Some species enjoy living together
They love worm poo. In some places, mud daubers have been seen visiting worm casts to collect their building materials.
Naked Mole-Rats spend virtually all their lives underground in complete darkness.
Around 1 in 500 narwhal males have two tusks. A tusk grows from the front right side of the upper jaw.
Climate change is the primary threat to narwhals.
The numbat is an Australian mascot and is regarded fondly in Australian culture.
They have no pouch. Unusually for a marsupial, female numbats do not have a pouch for their young.
Ocelots love to swim. Unlike many cats, ocelots are very comfortable in the water.
Their feet double as hands. With opposable big toes, orangutans can grasp branches, fruits, and much more with their feet.
Orangutans are among the closest relatives to humans. They share almost 97% of their DNA with humans.
They sleep in beds. At night, orangutans will make nests that function like mattresses up in trees.
Orangutans can laugh. They show laughter-like vocalisations when wrestling, play fighting, chasing, or tickling.
Orangutans are the only great ape found in Asia. Gorillas, chimpanzees, orangutans, and humans are all considered great apes.
The third species was discovered very recently. The Tapanuli species was discovered in 2017.
They have long, thick eyelashes that serve a purpose, despite being beautiful to look at.
They’re an umbrella species, so efforts to conserve one species can inadvertently protect others around it.
They’re probably tasty. The local people of Papua New Guinea eat a lot of insects.
They mate for life. Maras are life-long monogamists and may raise one to three offspring each year.
Penguins have built-in camouflage through the countershading of their plumage.
Piranha need warm water to survive, rather than warm blood.
Platypuses are venomous; males have an ankle spur that delivers venom strong enough to kill dogs and cause excruciating pain in humans.
They don’t have stomachs; like fish, they lack an acid-producing stomach, and their gullet goes straight to their intestines.
Baby platypuses are called “puggles.” They hatch from their eggs using an egg tooth and are completely blind and hairless.
Polar bears have a short mating window: their mating season runs from late March to May.
The polar bear’s Latin name, “Ursus maritimus,” means “sea bear,” fittingly, as polar bears spend their lives on sea ice.
Mantises are a group of insects containing over 2,400 species, found all over the world.
Praying mantises can rotate their heads 180 degrees.. This gives them a great range of vision for hunting.
Females are larger than males, measuring 7-9cm compared with males’ 6-7cm; females also live longer!
They have 3 stages of development: egg, nymph (adolescence), and adult.
They have one ear, located on the underside of their stomach.
Prehensile-tailed porcupines have a large range. While these are slow-moving rodents, they still get around!
They are some of the best swimmers and divers.
Large-nosed monkeys have nine stomachs, which is why they are known as the cows of the primate world.
Male proboscis monkeys have a bright red penis and a perpetual erection, a unique feature of this species.
They have excellent eyesight. Pufferfish are ambush predators which detect their prey using pin-sharp vision.
Pufferfish are popular in the aquarium trade, but few are captive-reared because raising them is extremely difficult.
Puffins are known to use tools. Atlantic puffins in Wales and Iceland have been observed using sticks to scratch themselves.
They are faithful partners. Puffins form long-term bonds and mating pairs remain together for their entire lives.
They have ultraviolet coloration. Puffins have recently been found to have photoluminescent patches on their bills.
Their beaks function like radiators. Highly energetic flight generates a huge amount of heat within puffins’ bodies.
Puffins are still hunted for their meat, eggs, and feathers.
Quokkas belong to the Macropodidae family, which includes kangaroos, wallabies, and pademelons.
They can climb trees. Although quokkas primarily walk, they can climb trees and shrubs to find food and escape from predators.
Quokkas are nocturnal. They spend the evening and night hours browsing for food and sleep during the day.
They’re excellent swimmers. Despite being land animals, raccoons are deft swimmers.
They’re toilet-trained: they don’t just poo anywhere; raccoons pick latrine sites and stick with a few of them.
Raccoons douse their food. ‘Lotor’ from their scientific name ‘Procyon lotor’ is Latin for ‘washer’.
They’re named for instruments. Both the rattlesnake’s common name and scientific name come from instruments.
Common kingsnakes are immune to their venom. Not everyone is at risk when it comes to the rattlesnake’s venom, however.
They brumate in large numbers
Their fangs are hinged, extremely sharp, hollow, and connected to venom ducts.
Rattlesnakes consume prey head first. This allows limbs and wings to fold at their natural joints so they can be swallowed.
Rattlesnakes, like other snakes, do not need to eat every day.
Like giant pandas, red pandas love bamboo and can consume almost four pounds a day.
Red pandas can stand on their hind legs, sometimes almost waving, when asking for food in zoos.
Red pandas belong to the order Carnivora but are not carnivores.
Red pandas can produce their body weight in poop in a single week.
The Firefox browser is named after the red panda, also called a firefox; Firefox confirms this.
The red-lipped batfish get their name from Darwin.
The white rhinoceros is the world’s most abundant rhino subspecies; there are two subspecies.
Adult rosy maple moths do not have mouths.
Female sailfish can lay 4.5m eggs; most of this tremendous amount of caviar is eaten by plankton feeders.
Female sarcastic fringeheads choose males with the most desirable homes as mates.
The sarcastic fringehead’s famous bad temper serves a purpose and is not simply grumpiness.
Geckos are masters of disguise, with amazing camouflage abilities.
Geckos have large eyes without eyelids to protect them.
A gecko’s sex can be identified as soon as it hatches.
Sawfish use their famous rostrum primarily to incapacitate and capture prey.
Scorpions sting around 1.5m people annually, with approximately 2,600 deaths.
Some species slow their metabolisms when food is scarce, helping them cope with limited food sources.
Meerkats hunt scorpions by biting off their stingers and are mostly immune to their venom.
Like crabs and spiders, scorpions grow and develop through cycles of molting.
A substance in scorpion skin makes them fluoresce under ultraviolet light, causing them to glow.
Monogamous pairs and sometimes familiar groups hunt together.
They lay 2-3 blue-green eggs, which are a few inches long and hatch after 45 days of incubation.
Sadly, the secretary bird is endangered because its grassland habitats are cleared and used for cattle.
Siberian tigers are highly revered; many cultures see tigers as heavenly spirits.
Sichuan takin young have darker coats at birth, helping camouflage them from predators.
Takin have four subspecies: Sichuan, Bhutan, Mishmi, and Golden.
Baby skunks are known as kits. They mate in early spring and give birth around May to litters of 4-7.
Newborn skunks are vulnerable, furless, and have their eyes shut.
Sloth hair is coated in thick, green algae that helps with camouflage.
Two-toed sloths can have as few as five cervical (neck) vertebrae.
Sloths are heterothermic and can be cold- or warm-blooded depending on their surroundings.
Sloth hair grows upside down; most mammals' follicles point downward, channeling water toward the ground.
Around 10,000 years ago, a five-ton, six-meter giant cousin of modern sloths disappeared.
The slow loris has two types of tongue, each with a different purpose.
They have short, stubby tails hidden beneath their dense fur.
Many species of Mystrium ant can lie motionless when disturbed, playing dead until the threat has passed.
Solenodons are among the few remaining mammals that can carry and inject venom.
Solenodons have glands in their armpits and groin regions that release a musky, goat-like odor.
Female solenodons have nipples more or less on their buttocks.

Bats are the only flying mammals and comprise the second largest order of mammals in the world.
A bat's grasp is strong enough to hold its entire body weight while its body hangs upside down.

Botos have poor vision so they rely on their echolocation to navigate through the muddy waters and find food.

Bears can be found throughout most of the lowland and mountainous ranges in North America.

Vampire bats have fewer teeth than any other bat because they do not have to chew their food.
Along with flying, vampire bats can run, jump, and hop with great speed, using their chest muscles to fling themselves skyward.
Vampire bats in the same colony support their roostmates by regurgitating blood to bats that are unable to find food.
Vampire bats do not suck blood. They make a small incision and lap up the blood of their hosts.
An individual otter maintains a territory. Otters mark their territories with scent, and fervently patrol and defend their territories.
Clawless otters have very sensitive forepaws. They use their forepaws for foraging.

Crabeater seals belong to the scientific order Pinnipedia, which includes seals, sea lions, and walruses.
Seals differ from sea lions in a number of ways, including having no visible earflaps.

Wildebeests live in more densely packed herds than any other large mammal, except for humans.

Wildebeests are also called gnus because their call sounds like gnu gnu.
These bats cannot echolocate. Instead, they use their excellent sense of sight and smell to locate their food.

Anteaters detect insects with a powerful sense of smell, 40 times that of humans.
Anteaters sleep as much as 15 hours each day.
Giant anteaters are generally solitary, except for mother-and-young pairs.
Giraffes strip leaves from thorny acacia trees, sometimes colonized by ants, with their 45 cm (18 in.) long, prehensile tongues.
Bull giraffes forage higher in trees than cow giraffes, reducing food competition between the sexes.
Giraffes have seven elongated neck vertebrae, unlike most mammals; this adaptation helps them feed high in trees.
Each giraffe has unique markings that distinguish it from others, much like a human fingerprint.
Giraffe markings allow them to hide among trees.

Seals differ from sea lions in several ways, including having shorter, stouter flippers and no visible earflaps.

Although koalas may look like bears, they are actually marsupials, animals with a pouch that protects developing young.

Leopard seals belong to the scientific order Pinnipedia, which includes seals, sea lions, and walruses.
The leopard seal is named for its spotted coat pattern.
Leopard seals have uniquely shaped cheek teeth that allow them to strain krill out of seawater.

Malayan flying foxes are one of the world's largest bat species.
Contrary to their scientific name, Malayan flying foxes are not vampire bats. They are fruit-eaters.
Flying foxes have long, sharp, curved claws on their toes, allowing them to hang effortlessly upside down in trees.
Malayan flying foxes do not have the ability to echolocate but can use their sharp vision to locate prey.
Bats are the world's only flying mammals. Other mammals may glide through the air, but bats flap their wings and fly.
Malayan flying foxes are mostly nocturnal, flying up to 60 km (36 mi) a night while foraging.
Hawaiian monk seals are considered one of the world's most critically endangered mammal species.
Narwhals and Belugas are the only members of the family Monodontidae.

Ross seals belong to the scientific order Pinnipedia, which includes seals, sea lions, and walruses.
Ross seals are known to make distinctive warbling and trilling calls in displays to attract mates and as a threat.
The deepest dive recorded for the species was 792 m, while the longest dives were over 30 minutes in duration.
In Ernest Hemingway's book, The Green Hills of Africa, he describes the habits of sable antelope that he hunted.

Sitatungas are one of the most aquatic antelope species and have many adaptations that allow them to spend time in water.
Sitatunga coats are shaggy and several inches long; their thin hair is also coated with an oily, water-repellent secretion.
These antelope use regular, tunneled pathways through tall reeds and papyrus to navigate through the swamps.
Sitatungas are excellent swimmers, able to paddle slowly for several miles, often coming to rest on dry mounds or floating vegetation.
Sitatungas will wait until dark to leave the swamp and graze at the edge of nearby forests.
